October 15 — Functionality, faster maps and lower data transfer

The user prioritizes useful capabilities and measured outcomes. Reference links
help identify gaps; they are not the product structure or a requirement to copy
particular tools. Current publication and Catalog releases continue independently.

Fast GeoParquet→PMTiles processing is a priority: avoid a large intermediate
GeoJSON/FlatGeobuf export, preserve canonical data and derived lineage, and make
maps responsive with fewer transferred bytes. Start with the existing direct GDAL
recipe. Verify one representative bounded conversion and map read, recording
conversion time, peak memory/scratch space, source/output bytes, actual map range
bytes and geometry/attribute/ID losses. Evaluate an alternative only if it improves
that outcome. No global backfill, speculative engine replacement or always-on
processing follows from this requirement.

| Capability | Existing foundation | Remaining functional requirements |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 3D terrain inspection and measurement | Governed renderer terrain; saved paths, profiles, gain/loss and sampled 3D distance | Connected DEM/DTM/DSM points/surfaces, DEM cross-sections and contours, imagery/hillshade comparison and quality inspection. Keep NoData gaps open; display interpolation must not alter analytical elevations. |
| Imagery discovery and processing | [STAC workflow](stac-services.md), selected Sentinel-2 search, reference registration, temporary COG preview, governed raster jobs | Landsat bbox/date/band search and composites, deliberate processing estimate/job/cancel/result delivery as a derived Layer. Preserve provider references and per-distribution terms. |
| Embedding analysis and classification | [Array/reference direction](stac-services.md#tessera-reference-and-analysis-direction) | Bounded annual embedding windows, labels, similarity/classification, stronger MLP and explicit spatial train/validation/test separation, geographic/field-grouped splits and leakage reports. Reuse published embeddings; training remains a deliberate bounded job. |
| Code, Python and data exploration together | Notebook/code authoring, maps/charts, remote COGs and saved Jupyter output rendering | Executing Python/kernel and variable inspection, LAS/LAZ/COPC display/measurement, integrated maps/plots. Saved Jupyter outputs are not execution; buildings/canopy are not a point-cloud viewer. |
| Large spatial/time catalogs | Partition inventories, pruning, bounded Parquet ranges, cancellation/deduplication, PMTiles and Catalog reuse | Generic temporal exploration, reference catalog integration and representative scale/latency acceptance; deliberate refresh schedules and explicit upstream freshness. Avoid duplicate mirrors or unrequested backfills. |
| Reusable cartographic layouts | [Print Studio plan](print-studio.md), positioned maps/furniture/content, bounded PNG/PDF/SVG | Durable Project layouts, independent insets, guides/snapping, grids/ticks, legends/scales/north arrows, continuous/negative-value colorbars and wider palettes, LaTeX/tables/charts, styling/order, raster series/NDVI/NBR+, high DPI, vector PDF, GeoPDF and QGIS templates. Verify each export separately. |
| Remote scientific raster access | Referenced COG registration and deliberate HTTP ranges | Signed/float values, actual provider/CRS/compression/CORS acceptance, including England DTM versus first-return DSM and EPSG:27700. Preserve authority, attribution and accuracy metadata. Current CRS support is 4326/3857/WGS84 UTM. |

The direct conversion implementation is `pmtiles_derivatives.py`, with a sealed
no-egress GDAL job, bounded area/zoom, API admission up to 512 MiB and a separate
worker file ceiling of 2 GiB. Command fixtures use placeholder bytes; the selected
local acceptance uses a real retained GeoParquet file. The current optional Rust
candidate is tylertoo; retain its version, staging, geometry/property and memory
limits in the implementation audit, and adopt it only for measured benefit.
Reference-source details stay in the existing checkpoint rather than driving the
product structure. The priority remains quick conversion followed by an actual
usable tiled map, with measured transfer and explicit preservation limits.

The real local case now converts23,888 bytes of GeoParquet to14,855 bytes of PMTiles
in 0.737 seconds including validation; one diagnosed staging-name failure brings
cumulative conversion time to 1.055 seconds. The existing reviewed protocol decodes
six tile features with one 14,855-byte local read and caches the reread. This tiny
correctness case is not a large-data speed/transfer benchmark. All 41 non-null
property records survive, five nulls are omitted, row FIDs are not retained, and
zoom 8 simplification/quantization has sampled deviations up to123.04 metres.
Canonical input is unchanged; tile fragments are not source row counts.

## Lambda lifecycle and live release baseline resolved — September 26

never invoked ASGI lifespan, so the preceding pooling edit alone would not activate
on Lambda. The adapter now explicitly owns the application lifespan inside each
invocation's event loop and closes it on failure too. Reuse is within a Lambda
request, not across separately created loops. Thirty-three focused Lambda/Data API
tests pass; no image build/deploy. [Local checkpoint](../.local-archives/data-api-pooling-20260926/checkpoint.json).

One bounded production read confirms the retained API digest is still active. All
12 migrations 0139–0150 are absent. First SQL call returned DatabaseResumingException
after 4.593 seconds; after one 15-second wait, read succeeded in 0.535 seconds.
Three explicit AWS calls, zero writes; credential-provider calls not separately
measured. Approval-review timeout and missing-script launch both preceded AWS work
and are retained. This demonstrates a paused database, not complete browser-auth
latency or a need for always-on compute. [Receipt](../.local-archives/progress-runtime-20260919/native-input-b58a62893c16/live-baseline-20260926.json).

Next: finish compatibility review of missing migrations and source-archiver runtime,
then prepare one current API input and bundle schema/image release through existing

completion path now resolve the version/job by the upload intent's recorded completed
original object. Missing original identity fails closed. Current dataset presentation
is still returned independently; no object reinspection, mutation or current-pointer
change occurs during retry.

The ingestion regression now supplies a newer current version while retrying the older
upload and checks the original object query, returned original version/job and no
storage read/write. All 29 ingestion checks and Ruff pass. An accidental extra keyword
on the ordinary completion read was caught and corrected before the passing rerun.
No cloud/provider/build/CI. Checkpoint:
`.local-archives/upload-exact-completion-20260923/checkpoint.json`.

Staged upload creation remains next: retain the existing Layer/current version, bind

are arbitrary ordered groupings of Projects and/or Publications in an Account,
with private, account-visible or public visibility. Optional metadata does not
make a Collection a tag. Existing private identity, grants, member references and

## Geographic-tag implementation status

The user's latest correction supersedes earlier independent-Location classification
and hierarchy priorities. Geography is continuous; tags provide optional named
context, not an exhaustive partition or definitive containment. No resource creation,
inspection, composition or publication should require creating a Location first.
Existing persistence remains useful for reusable tags, provenance and access.

The current increment aligns instructions and visible language while retaining
`/locations`, `view=locations`, IDs, revisions, grants and relationship history.
Layer-side geographic-tag read/add/remove now passes connected local acceptance
using versioned dataset metadata and standard place identities. Studio now resolves,
retains and reopens bounded Wikidata context through the local connected path.
Ancestor-aware canonical catalog discovery now passes connected local acceptance
using active place associations and retained typed edges. Explicit place-name
selection/disambiguation and per-result place-match explanations now pass the
connected local workflow. Shared Project/Publication/Collection knowledge now has
local persistence/authorization evidence. Collections now use the shared Studio
editor with connected metadata save/reopen and host draft guards. Project and
Publication hosts remain open after the Collection visibility batch. Preserve the audited authority boundary: editing a tag plus reading a target currently permits a link
from the tag; it must not silently authorize changing the target's own metadata.
Support many tags and overlapping/unknown geometry without required parents.
Retain current authorization on historical reads and inaccessible references.

Bundle the affected interface build and connected persistence journey after that
functional increment. Then resume the scoped parity-drift ownership queue. Existing
local Location receipts describe the predecessor behavior; no new parity or deployed
acceptance is established by terminology changes.

## Product acceptance journey

Discover a useful spatial source → remember it → preserve metadata and rights →
inspect the Layer → reuse it in several Projects → apply reusable Styles → compile
a Project Artifact → embed it in a Publication → organize Projects and Publications
in a Collection → share through an Account → return without reconstructing the work.

Publications may also use Layers directly for prose, analysis, charts, code and
custom rendering. A Project is not a prerequisite for every publication visualization.

Use three tests for each increment:

1. **Connect once. Curate once. Reuse continuously.**
2. **Preserve first. Normalize second. Enrich transparently. Never erase provenance.**
3. **Context should accumulate without collapsing ownership or data boundaries.**

## Domain contract and compatibility

| Concept | Product meaning | Existing implementation / next boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog | Known spatial resources, including remote resources | Dataset inventory is canonical; saved map presentations remain a separate compatibility view. |
| Layer | Persistent reusable resource, independent of Projects | `app.datasets` identity; `/layers?id=<uuid>` shell; project links do not transfer ownership. Upload is only one source pathway. |
| Style | Reusable rendering rules compatible with one or more Layers | Adapt existing style assets/version bindings, retaining map-specific configuration. |
| Project | Authored map/spatial composition referencing Layers and Styles | `app.maps` owns compositions; `app.projects` is a legacy access/work container. Establish an adapter before migrating identities/routes. |
| Artifact | Compiled Project output, with explicit current or immutable-version reference | Reuse existing render jobs and portable exports; common manifest and dependency contract remain open. |
| Publication | Independent editable narrative/analytical work | Existing governed pages, sites and Observable sources are building blocks; a published edition is output, not the Publication identity. |
| Collection | Arbitrary ordered Project/Publication references in an Account; private, account-visible or public | New independent identity; never reinterpret every existing client/container as a Collection. |
| Account | Organizational context with memberships and roles | Build on organizations and existing account switching. Creator, owner, permissions and membership remain distinct. |
| Geographic tag (legacy Location) | Optional reusable geographic context across resources | Retain Location IDs, routes, geometry, knowledge and links. No required boundary, hierarchy or classification; resource-side tagging is the next interaction. |

Known resources can be referenced, hosted, cached, mirrored, derived or locally
created. Record custody explicitly. A preview performs reads; it does not create
a Project, ingest bytes or grant rights. AWS supplies durable metadata, relationships,
objects and artifacts without replacing authoritative remote services.

The knowledge model must cover identity and canonical URI; provider/publisher/creator;
source/current endpoints and retrieval time; access method/authentication and
cache/redistribution conditions; license/copyright/attribution/access rights/policy;
distributions and service/download URLs; spatial and temporal characteristics;
nested attribute semantics, units and vocabulary URIs; transformation lineage;
and contextual relationships with evidence and confidence. The current assertion
schema is a foundation, not finished dedicated UI for all of these fields.

Preserve source documents and normalized, curated and inferred assertions separately.
Keep unknown explicit; retain evidence, method, timestamp and agent/version. Do not
promote inference to source authority or apply dataset terms to every distribution.
Use standards vocabulary and explicit projections: DCAT 3/DCTERMS, JSON-LD,
OGC API – Records, STAC, PROV-O and ODRL as appropriate. Descriptive permissions
never substitute for application authorization or establish automated policy compliance.

## Ordered execution register

“Local” means implemented with bounded local checks, not deployed or end-to-end
accepted. “Partial” means existing primitives satisfy only part of the outcome.
No aggregate completion percentage is implied. Dependencies determine order; independent
local fixes can proceed while a release or external acceptance step is blocked.

| ID | State | Next outcome and acceptance boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RM-01 | Local baseline; audit ongoing | Audit Layer/Project/Publication across entities, routes, catalog, labels, payloads, creation and rendering. Initial findings are in the audit; repeat the affected portion before each domain migration. |
| RM-02 | Deployed search and paging; broader source workflows partial | Canonical dataset inspection, resource-first catalog and explicit Project reuse exist. Catalog picker and Data access workspace paging load 100-record pages deliberately; complete-inventory server search and the normal-session Search/empty/Clear journey pass. Exact-built keyboard/390px and stale account/disposal checks pass; the final-page focus handoff is a local follow-up for a future bundle. Hosted large-inventory latency and every-account acceptance remain open. Finish discover/connect outside a mandatory authored Project, source modes and authorized known uses. |
| RM-03 | Local bridge; full-stack acceptance open | Bridge existing governed metadata into canonical Layer inspection; preserve record identity, source representation, revision and authority without copying claims into a false level. Detailed slice below. |
| RM-04 | Owned guided persistence, queued discovery and inline Download/reopen hosted accepted; broader curation partial | Guided marker Save/fresh reopen/forward baseline restore/fresh reopen passes on owned Layer `45d748c9…`, retaining history and source/provider/license/rights/discovery bytes. This closes only the authenticated owned-Layer persistence subitem. Production >32 KiB object persistence, larger refresh, connector breadth and normalized projections remain required/open; continue remaining selected core workflow acceptance. |
| RM-05 | Local selection/save/reopen verified; broader acceptance open | Expose compatible reusable Styles and default/chosen versions from Layer inspection; use the same Layer and Style in two compositions, save and reopen without duplicating either resource. |
| RM-06 | Local identity/navigation/context baseline; wider acceptance pending | Separate authored Project and Publication identity/navigation from the legacy access container. Preserve old routes and authorization with adapters. Creation/preview must create only the intended entity; preserve existing map/site/source functionality. |
| RM-07 | Local image/portable identity, retention, current selection and reuse verified; release acceptance open | Common Project Artifact manifest, current pointer and immutable versions; portable HTML/JS/CSS/config with rights-aware network/offline dependencies. Pin data/style/config versions or declare live dependencies. Reuse unchanged input hashes and prevent uncontrolled build cascades. |
| RM-08 | Connected Artifact authoring/build reuse verified; helper Files Save/reopen hosted accepted; broader consumption open | AUTHOR-001C helper Review/Use/Save/fresh reopen preserves six exact files, approved rights and the existing source compiler pin; no new hosted helper build is claimed. Continue Publication identity, direct Layer consumption and explicit latest/pinned Artifact embeds; unavailable/revoked dependencies must fail honestly. |
| RM-09 | Mixed owner Collection visibility round trip hosted accepted; sharing/isolation open | Actual account-shell private → account → public → private saves/reopens revisions 4–6, preserves member IDs/resources, omits private/unlisted members publicly and returns anonymous 404 after restoration. Another account reader, explicit sharing/grant revocation, positive public-member inclusion and owner177 shared Layer/Style acceptance remain open. |
| RM-10 | Partial; authorization applies throughout | Extend existing Account membership/roles to new identities and Collection sharing. Verify one person switching accounts, explicit grants and no inferred ownership or cross-account leakage. Branding/storage/policy/billing remain separate later capabilities. |
| RM-11 | **Active**; versioned identity, Studio authoring/history, hierarchy, sharing and resource links verified locally; broader discovery and deployed acceptance open | Geographic tags carry names, optional coordinates/geometry and evidence. Preserve existing links and optional parent history; implement resource-side attachment with independent resource edit authority. No mandatory geographic classification. |
| RM-12 | **Active**; manual provider reference workflow implemented; discovery and verified retrieval open | Context discovery records provider observations and evidence/confidence. Start street imagery with provider-supported links/integrations; review current provider terms before implementation. Derived resources retain independent rights and lineage. |
| RM-Q1 | Local reliability fix | Metadata save must not race reload/discard or allow a late response to erase new edits. Hold editor controls during the request and preserve the draft on failure; verify deferred success/failure. |
| RM-V1 | Open; release lane | Real API/PostgreSQL authenticated save/reopen and multi-project reuse acceptance, then reviewed migration/API/Studio release and live verification. Local fixtures do not close this item. |
| RM-V2 | Open; maintenance lane | Resolve existing foundation inventory, evidence-seal and broad type-check failures with owning evidence. Never reseal unrelated files solely to obtain a green aggregate result. |

## RM-03 implementation and remaining acceptance

Start in `services/api/src/opengeo_api/resources.py`, its governed-resource and
metadata routes, `ingestion.py`, `app/src/lib/apiClient.js`, `LayerResourcePage.js`
and `ResourceKnowledge.js`. Existing governed records and retained metadata-source
documents already exist; inspect their actual authorization and version semantics
before adding another storage mechanism.

1. Resolve accessible governed records linked to the canonical dataset, retaining
   their project/resource identity and revision. Dataset access alone must not
   expose metadata from a restricted governed resource or another account.
2. Present the existing records and their retained source/projection separately
   from new dataset knowledge assertions. Clearly label what was actually retained;
   do not call a canonicalized stored representation original provider bytes.
3. Use existing authorized read APIs where sufficient; otherwise add a bounded,
   tenant-scoped lookup. Avoid sequentially crawling every project from the browser.
   No provider fetch, automatic write, backfill or source-authority promotion on view.
4. Verify a Layer reused by two projects, restricted sibling records, tenant
   isolation, missing records, revision changes, nested/source-text preservation
   and reopening. Ensure the UI uses the same authorization path as the API.
5. Update this row and the audit with actual evidence and limitations. Continue to
   RM-04 rather than wait for unrelated cloud activation.

RM-03 now has a bounded `/datasets/{id}/resources` lookup, independent resource
permission filtering, and read-only governed-record/import-source inspection on the
Layer page. It does not copy claims or fetch providers. The current governed revision
and latest retained import version are labeled separately. Local SQL/API/component
checks and the bundled Studio build pass; full authenticated API/PostgreSQL browser
acceptance remains in RM-V1. RM-Q1 draft protection remains in place.

## RM-04 current increment and next implementation slice

Guided curation now shares one optimistic revision draft with the advanced JSON
editor. Users can append resource/distribution claims, explicit unknowns, curated or
inferred authority, evidence, confidence and method; describe distribution custody;
and retain user-supplied source text with retrieval context. Existing claims and
nested values remain intact. Pending form entries must be added or cleared before
save; clearing a form preserves the shared draft. No automatic provider response
capture is claimed by this UI increment.

Successful ArcGIS/WFS discovery retains exact response-body bytes up to 32 KiB
alongside the normalized result, with SHA-256, retrieval time and length. Migration
0153 adds governed immutable object references for successful bodies above 32 KiB
through 2 MiB. Deliberate Layer-authorized download checks exact version, length
and checksum. Small inline documents and historical size omissions remain compatible;
newer provider fetches cannot recover omitted original bytes. Failed/invalid responses
are not archived. Migration 0106 supplies immutable documents and registration links. Registration checks account/project/source/collection binding before writes;
Layer access governs subsequent linked inspection and checksum-verified download.
Older jobs remain usable with an explicit missing-document state. No additional
provider fetch or automatic rights interpretation occurs.

Normal authenticated queued discovery, reference-Layer registration and exact saved
inline-response Download/reopen now pass. Guided Layer knowledge Save/fresh reopen/
forward exact-baseline restore/fresh reopen also passes on owned `45d748c9…`,
retaining marker history and existing source claims. Only this owned persistence
subitem closes; continue remaining selected core workflow acceptance. Remaining
RM-04 breadth: production >32 KiB
private-object persistence, larger-document metadata refresh, other connector
families and provenance-aware normalized metadata projections. The registration form now starts its
terms-review entry blank, so a default never implies a completed review. These
gaps remain tracked while independent style integration proceeds.

## RM-05 local workflow and remaining acceptance

Layer inspection now exposes existing governed Style identities, versions and
checksum-verified definitions/downloads, with capability-gated version editing and
unsaved-draft protection. Format alone does not establish data/renderer compatibility.
The authorized exact-version API/client read now resolves immutable revisions
without falling back to latest. Explicit compatible Style-version bindings now
include dataset association and current access checks in composition saves. Exact-version inspection and immutable fixture history now
exist locally. The workbench now offers on-demand selection and verification of an
exact version for a governed vector composition. Preserve the distinction between a
reusable Style identity and a Project's chosen configuration/version. Reuse one
Layer and Style in two saved compositions and reopen both without copying data or
reconstructing styling. Keep explicit version selection and conflict handling;
missing/incompatible styles must not silently replace a saved presentation.

A staged backend 1.4 composition contract now validates exact `styleReference`
identity/version/checksum and dataset association with resource-read authorization
in the save transaction. Existing 1.3 maps/writes remain supported. Studio now resolves exact references when opening a governed vector composition,
verifies bytes and keeps unavailable or incompatible dependencies unrendered.
Style selection and explicit return to previous inline styling are now local. Private render-job submission now resolves
exact dependencies with current resource access and dataset/checksum checks. The
static compiler supports a bounded detached MapLibre geometry profile and rejects
unresolved/unsupported references without inline fallback. Publication audience
checks remain open. Bound compositions use current access checks and exact-version
resolution in browser/private static rendering; unsupported presentations fail
explicitly. The built workbench now saves/reopens two compositions through the real
local API/PostgreSQL runtime authorization path; token verification is substituted
and rendering disabled. Renderer/export/publication coverage remains open. Import rebinding remains
explicitly unsupported. See the API contract for the stored fields.

The offline PostgreSQL check now verifies two saved compositions sharing one dataset
and exact Style, reopening unchanged after a newer Style revision, revoked access
blocking resolution/save, and cross-tenant read denial. It applies all 106 migrations
and runs production repositories as the non-superuser, RLS-bound runtime role.
Run `services/api/.venv/bin/python scripts/check-style-reuse-postgres.py` only when
changed persistence/authorization behavior warrants it; it requires the cached
pinned PostGIS image and local container/socket access, and cleans up afterward.
Workbench permissions now preserve account-admin access even with a viewer project
role. Unavailable-Style save errors retain the draft and offer accurate recovery;
the component journey covers selection, rejected save and unchanged retry.
The `--browser` option now exercises actual built Studio controls through HTTP,
database identity lookup and runtime authorization. Both compositions select/save
and reopen revision 1 while revision 2 remains latest. The integrated check found
and fixed picker state loss during background Layer refresh. Token verification is
substituted and the renderer is disabled; this does not close RM-V1 or establish
deployment or rendering acceptance.

## Identity baseline and next implementation slice: RM-07

The authored-composition adapter is local: `GET /compositions/{id}` returns the
`app.maps` identity with explicit `access_container_id`, and Studio opens
`/workspaces?composition=<uuid>`. Resolution/read authorization share a transaction;
creation, Save as and map switching emit canonical links. Legacy URLs/APIs remain.
A built-browser/local-API/PostgreSQL journey verifies both URL forms save/reopen.

The Publication adapter is also local: `GET /publications/{id}` returns stable site
identity, explicit access context and the compatible site record after SITE_READ.
`/workspaces?publication=<uuid>` opens exactly that Publication, including when
several share one container. Directory source actions and successful creation emit
canonical links. Local built-browser/API/PostgreSQL evidence verifies exact
read-only reopening; tenant/revocation checks pass. Legacy publishing links remain.

The Projects directory now lists permission-filtered composition summaries via
`GET /compositions`, with server search/status filters and bounded pagination.
Access containers remain separately available as Access workspaces for data and
permission management. Empty/Publication-only workspaces are not shown as authored
Projects. Local browser/API/PostgreSQL checks verify listing, search and current
membership filtering; legacy URLs remain supported.

Composition titles and access-workspace management labels are now distinct.
Browser drafts are scoped by Account/user/composition within their access workspace;
ambiguous older drafts remain downloadable. Publication map previews use saved
state without restoring local drafts or temporary layers. Component/build evidence
covers this increment; wider live acceptance remains pending. Legacy access-workspace
lists still have a first-100 limit; the authored directory has independent pagination.

RM-07 now has an image manifest, durable identity/editions/current pointer and exact
completed-render reuse with connected local metadata acceptance. Portable browser
snapshots now have exact-byte manifests retained on Publication insertion, including
optional downloaded sidecars. Originating saved composition IDs, versions and
definition hashes are now preserved separately as browser-recorded provenance.
Portable checksum encoding now matches the server, with a legacy sidecar verifier.
Server byte/origin validators and migration 0109 portable object/edition bindings
and retained-output repository registration are local. Portable inspection/current
selection use the existing API and Studio controls with an explicit representation
selector. Retained-output finalization is exposed with exact-version reads; upload
intent/ticket and verified completion internals are local under migration 0110.
Bounded expired-intent cleanup and its exact-version AWS adapter are local.
Durable attempt/receipt records (0111) and explicit repository storage adapters are
local. Default-off application storage configuration and lifecycle routes are wired;
Studio upload/registration controls and real local PostgreSQL repository persistence
are verified with synthetic exact-byte storage. Browser/API/S3 and deployed acceptance
remain. Authorized portable output tickets and Studio download controls now pass
local PostgreSQL selection/revocation checks with synthetic signing. Next connect
durable current/pinned Publication references; do not store expiring download URLs.
Next attest saved Project build inputs and register portable
editions through durable persistence. Audit saved Project versus

## Validation and release handoff

The September 19 baseline has focused API tests, PGlite query/migration tests,
component tests, a seven-route Studio build and a built-browser check using fixture
identity/local HTTP substitution. See the audit for exact limits and prior receipts.
Migrations `0105_dataset_knowledge.sql` and `0106_discovery_metadata.sql`, plus API/worker/Studio changes, are local only.

Useful focused commands from repository root:

```bash
node --test app/test/resource-knowledge.test.mjs app/test/layer-resource-page.test.mjs
services/api/.venv/bin/pytest services/api/tests/test_resource_knowledge.py services/api/tests/test_ingestion.py services/api/tests/test_ingestion_routes.py -q
node --test database/test/resource-knowledge.test.mjs database/test/reusable-project-datasets.test.mjs
git diff --check
npm run foundations:check
npm run parity:check
```

Choose tests appropriate to each change; run a Studio build when route/build changes
warrant it. Do not repeat the whole baseline for documentation-only updates.
The built browser harness is `app/scripts/check-resource-knowledge-local.mjs`;
it needs a current Studio build and local loopback permission.

For a release, validate migration order and recovery in the target environment,
apply 0105/0106 before the compatible API/worker and then Studio, then verify actual account access and
reopen behavior. Rollback retains immutable metadata history; older applications
may ignore the new table. Do not infer deployment authorization from this plan.
Known broad failures remain tracked in RM-V2, distinct from the focused local result.

## References and checkpoint discipline

Study QGIS, GeoLibre, GeoServer, PostGIS, ArcGIS, Observable and Jupyter for specific
interaction patterns. GeoLens is an additional reference for explicit source modes,
resource inspection and a separate map builder; the audit records what was inspected.
No wholesale recreation, dependency adoption or conformance claim follows from a reference.

At each coherent increment record changes, rationale, checks, remaining limits and
next ID here and in [progress](progress.md). Keep stable product definitions here,
implementation evidence in the audit, and operational details in their existing
contracts. Preserve older milestone exits and historical receipts; avoid maintaining
competing lists of the “next” task.

and `parity/geolens-comparison.json`. GeoLens 1.20.0 is pinned at commit
`f5fee67d8cf053d741590a00a55fe1870eed2b92` and integrated into the shared baseline.

including analysis, sharing and automation; all need GeoLens-specific acceptance.

acceptance requirements. RM-05 has bounded local built-workbench/API/PostgreSQL
save/reopen evidence. RM-06 identity/navigation is next; remaining live/rendering
acceptance stays explicit. The comparison expansion does not create a competing execution queue.
Scope is now eight products / fifty rows, with no new passing claim. The Studio welcome guidance and Why OpenGeo now explain connected tasks
and separate current entry points from planned Collections/Locations/artifact work.

All queue items follow the [cost-efficient evidence policy](../CONTRIBUTING.md#cost-efficient-implementation-and-parity-evidence):
coherent edit/build batches, few purposeful GitHub Actions runs, local evidence first,
bounded live-service budgets and cleanup, artifact reuse, low inactive cost and
measured latency. CI path scoping remains a separately reviewable optimization;
this documentation/UI increment dispatches no workflows or paid-service tests.
